        4.4     Flash Vote Discussion - Council Member, Suzanne Hyland.

Council Member Hyland stated that when the City contracted with FlashVote. the City Council
was assured that they would help develop a member panel that is representative ofthe community.
The goal in samplmg is to have a sample that represents the population, and the gold standard for
statistical surveys is a random sample. Y2K Analytics is expensive because they keep reaching
out to people until they have enough respondents to be representative of the population, which
takes a lot of time. FlashVote offered an alternate opportunity to develop a member panel that is
representative of the population. The FlashVote margin of error is higher than other methods
because the member pane1 is substituted for a random sample.

The reason the Council decided to fund the surveys was because they want trusted results, and
FlashVote assured them that this could be accomplished through the member panel. The smveys
are conducted quickly rather thari taking w_eeks like random sampling, but the tradeoff is accuracy.

Council Member Hyland discussed the member panel. Outreach had been conducted through the
community newsletter, Cmmcil eff011s, and other methods. The current member panel was
approximately 50% male and female. Age ranges were also equally represented with the exception
of age 30 and under, which FlashVote had indicated was not uncommon. Cottonwood Heights is
predominately an older community, so the current age distribution was representative of the
population. However, there was a discrepancy in district participation, with District 1 only having
approximately 16% participation and District 4 having approximately 35%.

Council Member Hyland initially spoke with FlashVote about either adjusting the member panel
or selecting participants to even out the differences between District 4 and District 1. However,
FlashVote wants all the raw data they can get. Instead of randomizing the member panel, their
solution was to weight survey data to balance representation across districts, which they indicated
would ensure equal influence from all districts regardless of population size. They arc developing
a dashboard that will enable the City to look at the results differently.

Flash Vote talked about helping the Council be better equipped to interpret survey data. To do so,
they would grant the Council early access to the beta version of the dashboard. Council Member

Cottonwood Heights City Council Meeting Minutes for June 2, 2026            Approved: _ _ _ _ __

                                                      13
Hyland was opposed to doing any additional surveys until the member bias could be addressed.
She also requested that FlashVote join the City Council virtually to explain the survey data so they
can be confident that there is no hias in how the data is gathered or reported.

Results of the first City survey were reviewed. Results can be fi ltered by ownership, district,
gender, etc., but the Member Panel filter is most important as it shows the number of member
responses versus others who accessed the survey through a forwarded link. The margin of error
was+/- 6%. As the member panel size increases, the margin of error should decrease. If FlashVote
could not deliver better results, she questioned whether the City should continue its contract with
them. Results can be very misleading unless you understand what they represent. For example,
in graph fmm, the ''other" responses seemed abnonnally high, but in reality, it was only 10 people
versus 273 panel responses.

Council Member Hyland was encouraged that FlashVote was willing to help the City better dial
in the data. When another survey is conducted, .she asked that they virtually attend a City Council
meeting to discuss how they addressed bias and-what results the Council should focus on. She
finds the free responses particularly insightful. Potential bias is not unique to Cottonwood Heights,
but she needs to feel comfortable that the data can he trusted and is reported in a fair and
representative way.

to be there very long, so it will no longer generate noise. The trail needs to be finished, and
Cottonwood Heights was lagging behind. Holladay and Millcreek were also behind, but the City
did not need to follow them.

                (Bv this resolution the Council will emphasize the anticipated upcoming severe

                would decrease water consumption and thereby ameliorate the severity of the
                anticipated drought).

Council Member Hyland thanked Public Works Director, Matt Shipp, for being proactive and
bringing the ordinance to the Council's attention. It would not be enforced initially, but it would

to all residents that the City is aware. There was quite a bit of pushback on Facebook for limiting
the splash pad to two days per week. However, it is not on a circulating system and is on its last
leg. The City is trying to do its part to conserve water and appreciates residents doing the same.

Greetings-

Drafts of the following items of legislation scheduled for consideration at the June 16th city council
business meeting are attached for your review and comment:

        1. Resolution 2026-34. By this resolution, the council will adopt the interim budget for FY 2026-27 as
           required under state law for entities proposing an increase to the certified tax rate. The interim
           budget will be circuiated separatety by Scott Jurges.

      2. ResSJlvtion 2026-35. By this resolution, the council will adopt the FY 2026-27 property tax impact
         schedule with updated information as required by state law for entities proposing an increase to
         the certified tax rate. The tax schedule will be circulated separately by Scott Jurges.

      3. Resolution 2026-36. By this resolution, the council will adopt a proposed tax levy and rate before
         22 June 2026 as required by state law, subject to further required actions including a truth-in-
         taxation hearing on 11 August 2026 or another date determined by the Salt Lake County Auditor.

      4. Resolution 2026-37. By this resolution, the council will approve and ratify a $259,786.50 bid and
         award a construction contract to Stapp Construction, Inc. for the work and materials to complete
         the city's project no. 25.009-Staker Way Drainage Improvements and 2700 East & De Ville Drive
         Roadway Improvements Project. A copy of the proposed construction contract also is attached.

        5. Resolution 2026-38. By this resolution, the council will approve and ratify a $298,166.55 bid and
           award a construction contract to Acme Construction, Inc. for the work and materlals to complete
      the city's project no. 006.26-3000 East & BCC Trail Crosswalk Project. A copy of the proposed
      construction contract also is attached.

  6. Resolution 2026-39. By this resolution the council will approve an amendment to a 2020
     agreement through Wasatch Front Regional Council concerning consulting services for an update
     of the city's general plan being performed by Landmark Design. The purpose of the amendment is
     to modify the scope of work anticipated to be needed to complete the subject general plan
     update. Copies of the proposed amendment agreement and its exhibits also are attached.

  7. Resolution 2026-40. By this resolution the council will approve the city's entry into an agreement
     whereunder Dunn Associates, Inc. will conduct an independent structural integrity assessment
     and preservation cost study of the historic "Old Mill" structure located at 6851 South Big
     Cottonwood Canyon Road in the city. Copies of the current draft of the proposed agreement and
     its exhibits also are attached. The agreement still is being negotiated as of this writing, however,
     so it's possible that changes may be made to it before the June 161~ meeting. If they are material,
     I'll alert you in the June 16th work session.

  8. Resolution 2 0 ~ . The city's leased fleet of police and public works vehicles is rotated every
     approximately two years. Because the vehicle lessor (here, Zions Bank) requires the city to
     purchase the fleet at the end of the lease term, the city requires the original seller of the fleet
     (here, Garff Enterprises) to agree at the time of sale to repurchase the fleet at the end of the lease
     term, thereby insulating the city from the repurchase risk. This resolution will approve the city's
     entry into that repurchase agreement. Copies of the proposed agreement and its exhibits also are
     attached.

   9. Resolution 2026-42. This resolution will approve the city's entry into the lease of a new fleet of
      police and public works vehicles from Zions Bancorporation. Copies of the proposed
      lease/purchase agreement and its exhibits also are attached.

   1 o. Resolution 2026-43. By this resolution, the council will consent to the manager's appointment, re-
        appointment or re-assignment of several members of the city's planning commission.

   11. Resolution 2026-44. By this resolution, the council will consent to the manager's appointment of
       several members of the city's parks, trails and open space committee.

Please contact me as soon as possible inhere are any questions or concerns regarding the attachments.

Kind regards-

Shane

        A RESOLUTION OF THE CITY COUNCIL (THE "COUNCIL") OF THE CITY OF
    COTTONWOOD HEIGHTS (THE "()Jr") ADOPTING AN lNTERlM BUDGET (THE
 "]NTERJM BUDGET") PURSUANT TO UTAH CODE ANN. §59-2-924(8) THAT IS BASED
ON THE PROPOSED TAX RA TE rNCREASE AND IN EFFECT FOR THE PERIOD BEGINNING 1
JULY 2026 AND ENDING AFTER THE DATE ON WHICH THE COUNCIL, SUBJECT TO THE
   REQUIREMENTS OF UTAH CODE ANN' §59-2-919, ADOPTS A FINAL BUDGET FOR
FISCAL YEAR 2026-2027; AND SETTING ASIDE, fN A RESTRICTED BUDGET ACCOlJNT,
                 $3.5 MILLION OF BUDGETED PROPERTY TAX REVENUE.

       WHEREAS, on 5 May 2026, the Council adopted a tentative budget pursuant to UTAH
CODE ANN. § l 0-6-111 that includes a proposed increase to the City's certified tax rate to generate
additional budgeted property tax revenue for Fiscal Year 2026-2027 of $3 .5 million; and

       "WHEREAS, on 2 June 2026, the Council held a public hearing on the tentative budget
pursuant to UTAH Com: ANN. ~ 10-6-114; and

       \YHEREAS, the Council \Vill adopt a Final Budget for Fiscal Year 2026-2027 after
meeting the requirements of UTAH Corn:: ANN. §59-2-919, including receiving and considering
public input on the proposed increase to the City's ce11ified tax: rate, at a duly noticed public
hearing on I l August 2026, or such other date as approved by the Salt Lake County Auditor: and

        WHEREAS, UTAH CODE ANN. §59-2-924(8) states in part:
        . . .On or before June 30, afiscal _vear taxing entity that proposes an increase to the certified
tax rate for the ensuing fiscal year shall . .. prepare and adopt an interim budget that . .. is based
on the taxing entity's proposed rax rare increase,· . . . includes a property tax impact schedule,
subject to the requirements of Subsection (8){b); and . .. is in ej/ect for the period beginning on
July I and ending after rhe date on ,1,·hich the ra:xing entity, su~ject lO the requirements ofSection
59-2-919, adopts a budget . ... ; and

         WHEREAS, UTAH CODE Ai'm. §59-2-924(8) further states in pai1 that, as the Council
adopts the Interim Budget, the Council shall:
         . .. set aside, in a restric!ed h11dget account, an amount of the taxing entity 's general fimd
revenue that is no less than the amount of additional ad valorem tax revenue that would be
generated by the taxing entity 'sproposed tax rare increase . .. [and}. . .a taxing entity .. . may not
expend or otherwise obligate the revenue that the taxing entity sets aside in a restric1ed budget
account . .. Jbr a period beginning on July I and ending after the date on which the taxing entity,
.1·u~ject to the requirements ofSection 59-2-919, adopts a budget; and

       WHEREAS, the Council wants to adopt the Interim Budget, substantially in the form of
attached Appendix "A", that is based on the proposed tax rate increase and is in effect for the
period beginning l July 2026 and ending after the date on which the Council, subject to the
requirements of UTAH CODE ANN § 59-2-919, adopts a Final Budget for Fiscal Year 2026-202 7;
and
      WHEREAS, the Interim Budget adopted by the Council includes a Propetiy Tax Impact
Schedule prepared pursuant to UTAH CODE ANN. §§59-2-924(8) AND 59-2-9 l 9(4); and

       WHEREAS, the Council wants to set aside in a restricted budget account, as part of the
Interim Budget, S3.5 milJion of property tax revenue that would be generated by the proposed
property tax rate increase; and

       WHEREAS, the Council wants to direct that the City may not expend or otherwise
obligate the revenue the Council set aside in a restricted budget account for the period beginning
on 1 July 2026, and ending after the date on which the Council, subject to the requirements of
Section 59-2-9 l 9, adopts a Final Budget for Fiscal Year 2026-2027;

        l.      The Counci I hereby adopts the Interim Budget in substantially the fonn of attached

ANN. §59-2-924(8). The Interim Budget shall be based on the proposed tax rate increase and is in

suhject to the requirements of UTAH CODE ANN. §59-2-919, adopts a Final Budget for Fiscal Year
2026-2027.

        2.      The Council hereby sets aside, within the Interim Budget, $3.5 million, the amount
of additional property tax revenue generated hy the proposed increase to the City's certified tax
rate for Fiscal Year 2026-2027.

        3.     The Cmmcil directs that the City may not expend or otherwise obligate the budgeted
property tax revenue that is set aside in the restricted budget account for the period beginning on
l July 2026 and ending after the date on which the Council, subject to the requirements of UTAH
CODE ANN. §59-2-919, adopts a Final Budget for Fiscal Year 2026-2027.

        4.     The Interim Budget shall be available for public inspection on the City's website
and, during normal business hours, in the office of the City Recorder at Cottonwood Heights City
Hall, 2277 East Bengal Boulevard, Cottonwood Heights, Utah.

       This Resolution, assigned no. 2026-34, shall take effect immediately upon passage as
provided herein.

            THE COUNCIL PURSUANT TO UTAH CODE ANN. §59-2-924(8).

       WHEREAS, on 5 May 2026, the Council adopted a tentative budget pursuant to UTAH
CODE A"t-.""N. § I 0-6-111 that includes a proposed increase to the City's certified tax rate to generate
additional budgeted property tax revenue for Fiscal Year 2026-2027 of $3.5 million; and

       \VHEREAS, on 2 June 2026, the Council held a public hearing on the tentative budget
pursuant to UTAH CODE ANN. §10-6-114; and

       ·wHEREAS, the Council will adopt a Final Budget for Fiscal Year 2026-2027 after
meeting the requirements of UTAH CODE ANN. §59-2-919, including receiving and considering
public input on the proposed increase to the City's certified tax rate, at a duly noticed public

       '-''HE.REAS, as required by UTAH CODE ANN. §59-2-924(8), the City Budget Officer
prepared a Property Tax Impact Schedule: and

       ·wHEREAS, the Property Tax Impact Schedule prepared by the City's Budget Officer
included the requirements stated in UTAH CODE ANN. §59-2-924(8 ), and

       \\'HEREAS, the City Budget Officer presented the Property Tax Impact Schedule to the
Council on 5 May 2026 in the public meeting at which the City's tentative budget was first
presented to the Council as required by UTAII CODE ANN. §59-2-919(4); and

       \VHEREAS, the Property Tax Impact Schedule has been presented and made available to
the public at each public meeting held prior to 30 June 2026 at which the Council discussed the
City's proposed general fund budget for Fiscal Year 2026-2027 and was presented as a separate
document from all other budget documents: and

      WHEREAS, the Council, in duly noticed public meetings, has had the opportunity to make
changes to the Property Tax Impact Schedule prepared and presented by the City's Budget Officer;
and

        \VHEREAS, UTAH CODE ANN. §59-2-919(4) states in part:
        . . . On or before June 30, a.fiscal year taxing entity that proposes an increase to 1he certified
tax rate fur the ensuingfiscal year shall . .. prepare and adopt an interim budget that. . . includes
a property tax impact schedule, sukject to the requirements ofSubsection (8)(b) .. . ; and

        \\'HEREAS, the Council wants to adopt the Prope11y Tax Impact Schedule in substantially
the fonn of attached Appendix "A" and incorporate the Property Tax Impact Schedule into the
Interim Budget; and,

       WHEREAS, the Property Tax Impact Schedule specifies the following as required by
UT i\H CODE ANN. §59-2-924(8):
        (A) the approxJmare dollar amount of additional ad valorem tax revenue that would he
generated hy the proposed tax rate increase;
        (B) the approximate percentage increase in tax revenue based on the proposed tax rate
increase;
        (C} the approximate percentage increase to the amot1nt of property taxes paid on an
average res;dence per year as a result of the proposed tax rate increase;
        (D) the approximate percentage increase to the amount of property taxes paid on an
average commercial property per year as a result of the proposed tax rate increase; and
... for each department ofthe taxing entity whose budget would be affected hy the proposed tax
rare increase: ... owUne the budget increase or decrease to the department as a resulr of the
proposed lax rate increase; and ... articulate the operational impact to the department {l the
taxing enti(v approves the proposed tax rate increase; and

       WHEREAS, the Council wants to request the Salt Lake County Auditor (the "County
Auditor") to include the Property Tax Impact Schedule as pa1t of the County Auditor's notice
prepared and sent pursuant to Ur AH CODE ANN. §59-2-9 l 9. I to each owner of real property in the
City on or before 22 July 2026 who is listed on the assessment roll, and the Council understands
that the City is responsible for the added costs to include the Property Tax Impact Schedule in the

       NOW THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED by the City Council of the City of
Cottonwood Heights as follows:

form of attached Appendix "A".

        2.     The Council incorporates the Propetty Tax Impact Schedule into the Interim Budget

                                   RESOLUTION NO. 2026-36

         A RESOLUTION OF THE CITY COUNCIL (THE "COUNCIL") OF THE CITY
    OF COTTONWOOD HEIGHTS (THE "CITY") ADOPTING A PROPOSED TAX LEVY
     AND RA.TE PURSUANT TO UTAH CODE ANN. §59-2-912, ON REAL PROPERTY
      \\'lTHINTHE CITY MADE TAXABLE BY LAW FOR FISCAL YEAR2026-2027,
    SUBJECT TO FURTHER ACTION AS REQUIRED BY UTAH CODE ANN.§§59-2-919
    AND 59-2-924 AND DIRECTING THE CITY MANAGER OR DESIGNEE TO REPORT
     THE PROPOSED TAX LEVY AND RA.TE TO THE SALT LAKE COUNTY AUDITOR
           AND THE UT AH STATE TAX COMMISSION BEFORE 22 JUNE 2026.

       WHEREAS, on 5 May 2026 the Council adopted a tentative budget pursuant to UTAH
CODE ANN. § 10-6-111 that includes a proposed increase in budgeted property tax revenue for
Fiscal Year 2026-2027 of $3 .5 million; and

      WHEREAS, on 2 June 2026 the Council held a public heaiing on the tentative budget
pursuant to UTAH Cool::: ANN. § 10-6-114; and

        \VHEREAS, the Council will adopt a Final Tax Levy and Rate for Fiscal Year 2026-2027
after meeting the requirements of UTAH CODE ANN. §59-2-919, including receiving and
considering public input on the proposed increase to the City's certified tax rate, at a duly noticed
public hearing on l l Au!:,rttst 2026 or such other date as approved by the Salt Lake County Auditor;
and

         \VHEREAS, UTAH CODE ANN. §59-2-912 states inpa1i:
        . . .the governing body of each taxing entity shall before June 22 of each year . . adopt a
proposed tax rate, or, if the tax rate is no! more than the cer1(/1ed tax rate, a.final tax rate for the
taxing entity; and . .. reporl the rate and levy, and submit the statement required under Section
59-1-913 and any other information prescribed by rules of the commission jor the preparation,
review, and certification qf the tax rate, to the county auditor of the county in which the taxing
entity is located; and

        \VHEREAS, the Council wants to adopt a proposed tax levy and rate for fiscal year 2026-
2027 based on an increase in budgeted property tax revem,e of S3.5 million pursuant to UTAH
CODE Al'·iN. §59-2-912, understanding that adopting a final tax levy and rate is subject to further
action under UTAH CODE ANN.§§ 59-2-919 and 59-2-924; and

       \VHEREAS, the Council wants to direct the City Manager or designee to report the
proposed tax levy and rate to the Salt Lake County Auditor and Utah State Tax Commission before
22 June 2026 as required by UTAH CODE ANN. §59-2-912; and

       "'HEREAS, the Council wants to direct the City Finance Director or designee to input the
proposed tax levy and rate into the Utah State Tax Commission Certified Tax Rate System (Tax
Rate Summary 693) before 22 June 2026;

        NOW THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED by the city council of the city of Cottonwood
Heights as follows:

        l.      The Council hereby adopts a proposed tax Ie-vy and rate of _ _ _ _ on each
dollar of taxable valuation on real property within the city of Cottonwood Heights made taxable
by law for fi scal year 2026-2027. The proposed tax levy and rate of _ _ _ _ shall be credited
as revenue in the general fund generating an additional $3 .5 million of ongoing property tax
revenue for fis cal year 2026-2027. The adoption of a final tax levy and rate for fiscal year 2026-
2027 is subject to further action under UTAH CODE ANN. §§ 59-2-919 and S9-2-924 including
holding a duly noticed truth-in-taxation hearing on 11 August 2026 or such other date as
detennined by the Salt Lake County Auditor.

        2.    The Council expressly reserves the power and right to amend the proposed tax levy
and rate made herein as it may deemjust, proper and appropriate under Utah law.

       3.     The Council directs the City Manager, or designee, to report the proposed tax levy
and rate adopted by the Council to the Salt Lake County Auditor and the Utah State Tax
Commission before 22 June 2026.

       4.       The Council directs the City Finance Director or designee to input the proposed tax
levy and rate into the Utah State Tax Commission Certified Tax Rate System (Tax Rate Summary
693) before 22 June 2026.

       This Resolution, assigned no. 2026-36, shall take effect immediately upon passage as
provided herein.

       PASSED AND APPROVED this 16th day of June 2026.

The Contractor will commence the work required by the Contract Documents within ten (10)
calendar days after the date of the Notice to Proceed; and will complete the same within sixty (60)
calendar days after the date of the Notice to Proceed; unless the period for completion is extended
otherwise by the Contract Documents. Work shall be completed no later than I October 2026.

4.03   Liquidated Damages
Contractor and Owner recognize that time is of the essence as stated in Paragraph 4.01 above and
that O,wner \\~ll suffer financial loss if the Work is not completed within the times specified in
Paragraph 4.02 and 4.03 above, plus any extensions thereof allowed in accordance with Aiticle 11
of the General Conditions. The parties also recognize the delays, expense, and difficulties in valved
in proving in a legal or arbitration proceeding the actual loss suffered by Owner if the Work is not
completed on time. Accordingly, instead of requiring any such proof, Owner and Contractor agree
that as liquidated damages for delay (but not as a penalty), Contractor shall pay Owner $500.00 for
each day that expires after the time specified in Paragraph 4.02 above for Substantial Completion
until the Work is substantially complete After Substantial Completion, if Contractor shall neglect,
refuse, or fail to complete the remaining Work within the Contract Time or any proper extension
thereof granted by Owner, Contractor shall pay O\vner $500.00 for each day that expires after the
time specified in Paragraph 4.02 or 4.03 above for completion and readiness for final payment until
the Work is completed and ready for final payment.

required by the Contract Documents; and (3) Contractor's safety precautions and programs.

to be valid and binding upon Owner and Contractor, who agree that the Contract Documents shall
be reformed to replace such stricken provision or part thereof with a valid and enforceable

Thank you for our continued work together on the Cottonwood Heights General Plan. Per your
request, I have prepared a revised Contract Amendment proposal that addresses the work
anticipated to complete the update of the general plan.

Scope of Work
TASK 1: Subcommittee Deliverables & Public Engagement
   A. Synthesize goals and develop draft outline. Landmark has reviewed the goa Is and mission
       statements provided by the City Council and Planning Commission, comparing them
       against the current dra Ft of the general plan. It is estimated that approximately 35-40% of
       the current draft plan content remains relevant. Additionally, work needs to be done to
       remove redundancy and streamline the organization of the goals.
            i.  Landmark will work with Staff to develop a new outline for the general plan,
                incorporating the new goals. We will also refine any accompanying maps or other
                exhibits to supplement the draft outline and convey its intent, such as the future
                land use map.
           iL   Staff will review the plan outline with City Council and Planning Commission for
                approval prior to next steps. Any feedback received will be incorporated into the
                outline prior to the preparation of public engagement materials or draft plan writing.
   B. Public Engagement. The public engagement stage will present the revised outline, goals,
        and vision for the plan to the public for review and comment.
            i.  Online Storymap & Questionnaire. An on line webpage (StoryMap) will summarize
                and present the core ideas of the draft plan outline in a simple and graphic format,
                accompanied by a brief questionnaire for feedback.
           ii.  In-Person Open House. An in-person open house will be held at City Hall, either
                independently or tied to a City event, to collect feedback on the draft.
                       a. Landmark will prepare boards for the open house, outlining the prior
                            planning process, mission, goals, maps, and other key ideas for each
                            element of the plan.
                       b. It is anticipated that planning commissioners and council members will
                            attend the open house, engaging with residents on subcommittee topics.
                       c. Attendees will be directed to the online questionnaire and/or have paper
                            copy available.
          iii.  Marketing Materials. Landmark will provide digital nyers to be used to promote the
                online engagement through the City's website, social media, mailers, etc.,


                                                                                                            page
                                                                                                               1
TASK 2: Draft Plan Revisions
   A. Revised Draft Plan Outline. Landmark will share the results of the questionnaire and open
       house with City staff. who will decide if any goals/policies are to be amended. Landmark
       will revise the draft outline as appropriate and the revised outline will be returned to Staff
       for leadership approval before commencing on the draft plan.
   B. Draft Plan -Administrative Draft. Landmark will revise the draft plan to align with new plan
       outline. rewriting most narratives and providing additional detailed explanations for key
       concepts to support and be aligned with the mission statements and goals. It is anticipated
      that the Housing element will remain unchanged. the Water element will mostly remain the
       same, and that the Sustainability element will be eliminated as a standalone element, being
       incorporated into other chapters.
           i.   Landmark will deliver the Administrative Draft as a working Word copy, with text
                and key rigures/maps.
          ii.   City staff will work with leadership to refine and revise the draft as necessary to
                solidify its content before returning the copy to Landmark for layout.
   C. Draft Plan - Layout Draft. After receiving the revised text of the Administrative Draft from
       City staff, Landmark will implement the changes within the Layout Draft. preparing the plan
       for public review. This draft will utilize the previous graphic layout of the 2025 draft, but will
       include the updated text and revised maps and exhibits as a document that is graphically
       rich and well organized. Key city master plans wm be included as an appendix to the plan.

TASK 3: Adoption Process
   A. Final Plan Revisions. After submitting the Layout Draft. it is assumed that the draft will be
       delivered from City staff to leadership for final review and comment. Based on the
       feedback received from leadership, which at this stage is assumed to be minor, Landmark
       will make final revisions to the plan prior to adoption.
   B. Adoption Hearings. Landmark will present the Flnal Draft General Plan to the Planning
       Commission and City Council at one meeting each for approval/adoption.
   C. Final Deliverables. Incorporating any final edits from the adoption hearings, Landmark will
       deliver the final plan (PDF) and all working files to the City and WFRC for project closeout.

Schedule

below. This schedule assumes regular coordination with Staff and the timely review of documents.

                      Agreement for Consulting Services
                                   ("Old Mill" Structure)

        THIS AGREEMENT FOR CONSULTING SERVICES (this "Agreement") is made
effective 16 June 2026 by COTTONWOOD HEIGHTS, a Utah municipality whose address is
2277 East Bengal Blvd., Cottouwood Heights, UT 84121 ("City"), and by DUNN
ASSOCIATES, INC., a Utah corporatiou whose address is 380 West 800 South, Suite l 00, Salt
Lake City, UT 84101 ("Consultant"). Iu this Agreement, City and Consultant are each a "party"
and collectively are the "parties."



       A.        City desires to obtain from a qualified professional provider an independent
stmctural integrity assessment and preservation cost study of the historic "O!d Mill" strncture
located at 6851 South Big Cottonwood Canyon Road in City (the "Old Mill"), and related
services, all in accordance with applicable standards and attached Exhibit A.

        B.      Consultant regularly provides such services and on or about 22 April 2026
submitted a proposal (the "Proposaf') to City in response to a request for statement of
qualifications (the "RFQ") issued by City on or about 26 March 2026 concerning the services
desired by City. Consultant subsequently submitted a scope of services and fee summary (the
"Summary"). Copies of tbe Proposal and the Summary are attached hereto as Exhibit "A"
("Exhibit A")

        C.      After reviewing Exhibit "A" City has determined to retain Consultant to provide
the subject services.

        D.     By this Agreement, City desires to retain Consultant, and Consultant desires to be
retained by City, to perform the subject consulting services 011 the terms and conditions specified
herein.

        E.      City and Consultant intend to ideutify herein the consulting services to be
performed for City by Consultant, the basis of compensation for sucb services, and to otherwise
set forth their entire agreement concerning the subject services. Consequently, this Agreement
shall supercede any and all prior or contemporaneous negotiations and/or agreements, oral and/or
written, between the parties concerning the services to be provided under this Agreement.



        NOW, THEREFORE, in consideration of the premises, the mutual covenants and
undertakings of the parties hereto, and for other good and valuable consideration, the receipt and
sufficiency of which is hereby acknowledged, the parties hereby agree as follows:

        Section 1.      Scope of Services. Consultant shall provide the services (collectively, the
"Services") to City that are described in the RFQ, Exhibit A, and below, as well as all ancillary
and associated services as may be reasonably necessary or advisable to complement and
complete tbe Services, all as contemplated by all applicable legal requirements and best
practices. Subject to the foregoing, the Services shall include the following :
               (a)    Phases. The Services shall be divided into the phases, steps and work
described in Exhibit A.

               (b)     JJublic Meetings. Consultant shall attend such public meetings concerning
the Services as City reasonably may direct. City shall schedule and advertise all public meetings
or hearings. City may request Consultant to make a presentation concerning the Services at one
or more public meetings to receive public input and direction from City. All reasonable and final
adjustments to the resulting Services as directed by City will be made by Consultant prior to their
final acceptance by City.

               (c)    Schedule. It is anticipated that the Services will be completed by 15
September 2026. Consultant shall comply -with such performance schedule for the component
Services as City reasonably may direct upon reasonable prior notice to Consultant.

              (d)      Deliverables. Consultant shall provide all deliverables identified in
Exhibit A as well as such other documentation and deliverables as City reasonably may request.

               (e)     Other Services. Consultant shall perform all other services and work as
specified in Exhibit A or as may be muntally agreed to by the parties in v,,rriting.

If Consultant believes that any of the aforementioned services merit payment of any additional
fee beyond the Fee (defined below), then Consultant shall so inform City in advance before
undertaking any of such additional services, describing the need for such additional services and
the not to exceed cost of providing them. If City desires Consultant to proceed with any such
additional services, City shall so inform Consultant in v,,rriting. Consultant may not perform any
additional services, or seek compensation therefor, without City's prior written consent.

        Section 2.      Performance of Services. Except as otherwise provided in this
Agreement, Consultant shall furnish all supervision, personnel, labor, materials, supplies and
shall obtain all licenses and permits required for performance of the Services. The Services shall
be perfonned at Consultant's offices and other mutually-agreeable places.

        Section 3.    Compensation; Invoices; Remittance. For satisfactory performance, City
shall pay to Consultant the maximum amount of $18,500.00 as detailed in Exhibit A (the "Fee").

               (a)    Invoices. Consultant shall invoice City for the Services performed during
each calendar month at the end of that month or as soon as practical thereafter. All invoices
submitted to City shall contain references to this Agreement. lnvoices shall detail the Services
performed and shall contain copies of all supporting documents or proof of any expenditures on
behalf of City, and otherwise shall be in sync with the stage of completion of the Services
outlined in Exhibit A. Invoices for any approved additional services also shall specify the
person(s) perfonning such services, the hours worked, the applicable hourly rnte(s),

                (b)      Questioned Charges. Any questions or objections by City concerning
Consultant's charges under an invoice for the any services shall be submitted within 30 days
after City's receipt of the subject invoice.

              (c)   Remittances. Subject to subsection 3(b), all invoiced amounts due for
Services performed shall be paid by City within 30 days after City's receipt of the subject
invoice. If payment is not remitted or reasonably questioned to Consultant when due, Consultant
shall be entitled to recover interest thereon at the rate of ten percent (10%) per annum from and
after the date the remittance is due and payable.

        Section 4.    Change in Level of Services. City shall be freely entitled to modify
(increase or decrease) the level of the Services hereunder by providing at least 15 days' prior
written notice to Consultant of such change, provided that any increase in the level of the
Services shall be subject to Consultant's reasonable approval. Consultant's compensation shall
be reasonably modified in connection with any such change.

        Section 5.     Suspension of Services. City shall have the absolute right to terminate the
Services at any time without cost or liability to Consultant as provided in this Agreement. City
also may by written notice direct Consultant to temporarily suspend performance of any or all of
the Services for a specified period of time. lf such suspension is not occasioned by the fault or
negligence of Consultant, this Agreement may be modified to compensate Consultant for extra
costs reasonably incurred as a result of said suspension, provided that any claim for adjustment is
supported by appropriate cost documentation and asserted within 20 calendar days after the date
that City issues an order for resumption of the Services. Upon its receipt of any such suspension
notice, Consultant immediately shall (a) discontinue the Services; (b) place no fmiher orders or
subcontracts in connection with the Services; (c) suspend all outstanding orders and subcontracts
in connection with the Services; (d) protect and maintain the existing work and work-product in
connection with the Services; and (e) otherwise mitigate City's costs and liabilities for the
suspended areas of the Services.

       Section 6.     Termination for Convenience. City may tem1inate this Agreement, or
any part hereof, at any time with or without cause prior to its completion by sending to
Consultant written notice of such termination. Upon auy such tem1ination, City shall pay to
Consultant the full amount due for all Services satisfactorily performed by Consultant as of the
date of termination, excluding damages or anticipated profits on work not yet completed or
perfonned.

        Section 7.     Ownership of Designs and Drawings. Consultant and its subconsultants
shall be deemed the authors and owners of the documents (including, without limitation, original
drawings, estimates, specifications, field notes and data, and whether printed or stored on paper
or as electronic, magnetic, or digital infonnation) respectively produced by them in performance
of the Services (collectively, the "Instruments of Service"), and shall retain all common law,
statutory, and other reserved rights therein, including copyrights. Conditioned only on City's

                  INSURANCE REQUIREMENTS FOR PARTIES
            CONTRACTING WTTH THE CITY OF COTTONWOOD HEIGHTS

       The contracting party shall procure and maintain for the duration of the contract
insurance against claims for injuries to persons or damage to property which may arise from or in
connection with the performance of the work hereunder by the contracting party, his agents,
representatives, employees or subcontractors. The cost of such insurance shall be included in the
contracting party's bid.

       A.      MlNIMUM LIMITS OF INSURANCE.

       The contracting party shall maintain limits no less than:

               1.      Professional Liability: $2,000,000.00 combined single limit per claim and
in the aggregate for bodily injury, personal injury and property damage, including "tail
coverage" for at least one year after completion of all services.

                2.     Automohile Liability: $2,000,000.00 combined single limit per accident
for bodily injury and property damage. "Any Auto" coverage is required.

               3.       Worker's Compensation: Worker's compensation limits as required by
applicable law for all employees and other persons.

               4.     Commercial General Liability: $2,000,000.00 combined single limit per
occmrence for personal injury and property damage; $2,000,000.00 annual aggregate. Broad
Fann Commercial General Liability is required (ISO 1993 or better). Personal injury, premises-
operations, products-completed operation, independent contractors and subcontractors fire legal
liability and, when appropriate, coverages for explosion, collapse and underground (XCU)
hazards.

               5.     Excess T,iahility. $5,000,000.00.

       B.      DEDUCTIBLES AND SELF-INSURED RETENTIONS.

        Any deductibles (5% limit), self-insured programs or retentions must be declared to and
approved by the city of Cottonwood Heights (the "City"). At the option of the City, either: the
insurer may be required to reduce or eliminate such deductibles or self-insured retentions as
respect to the City, its officers, officials and employees; or the contracting party may be required
to procure a bond guaranteeing payment of losses and related investigations, claim distribution
and defense expenses.




                                                -I 0-
       C      NOTICE OF INCIDENT OR ACCIDENT.

        The contracting patty shall sgree to promptly disclose to the City sll incidents or
occurrences of accident, injury, and/or property damage covered by the insurance policy or
policies.

       D.      OTHER INSURANCE PROVISIONS.

       The policies are to contain, or be endorsed to contain, the following provisions:

                      General Liability and Automobile Liabihty Coverages.

                      (a)    The City, its officers, officials, employees and volunteers are to be
covered as additional insured as respects: liability arising out of activities performed by or on
behalf of the contracting party; products and completed operations of the contracting party;
premises ovmed, leased, hired or borrowed by the contracting pa1ty. The coverage shall contain
no special limitations on the scope of protection afforded to the City, its officers, officials,
employees or volunteers.

                       (b)     The contracting party's insurance coverage shall be a primary
insurance as respects to the City, its officers, officials, employees and volunteers. Any insurance
or self-insurance maintained by the City, its officers, officials, employees or volunteers shall be
in excess of the contracting party's insurance and shall not contribute with it

                       (c)     Any non-material failure to comply with reporting provisions of
the policies shall not affect coverage provided to the City, its officers, officials, employees or
voltmteers.

                     (d)    The contracting party's insurance shall apply separately to each
insured against whom claim is made or suit is brought, except with respects to the limits of the
insurer's liability.

               2.      Worker's Compensation and Employer's Liability Coverage.

                The insurer shall agree to waive all rights of subrogation against the City, its
officers, officials, employees and volunteers for losses arising from work perfonned by the
contracting party for the City.

               3.     All Coverages.

               Each insurance policy required by this clause shall be endorsed to state that
coverage shall not be cancelled before its expiration date except after thi1ty (30) days' prior
written notice (from the insurer) by first class mail, return receipt requested, has been given to
the City.

       E.      ACCEPTABILTTY OF INSURERS.

              Iusurnuce is to be placed with insurers with a Bests' rating of no less than A: VII,
unless approved by the Manager.
       F.      VERITlCATION OF COVERAGE.

               The contracting party shall furnish the City with certificates of insurance and with
original endorsements effecting coverage required by this clause. The certificates and
endorsements are to be signed by a person authorized by that insurer to bind coverage on its
behalf. The certificates and endorsements are to be on forms provided before work commences.
The City reserves the right to require complete, certified copies of all required insurance policies,
with all endorsements, at any time.

       G.      SUBCONTRACTORS.

                The contracting party shall include all subcontractors as insureds under its policy
or shall furnish separate certificates and endorsements for each subcontractor. All coverages for
subcontractors shall be subject to all of the requirements stated herein .

  Submitting Team                    Dunn Associates, Inc. is a consulting structural engineering firm headquartered in Salt Lake City, licensed in over40 states, with
  Du 11n Associates, Inc.            a secondary office in St. George. I am the CEO and I live in Cottonwood Heights - less than four miles from the Old Mill. I have
  Aegis Project Controls             walked past this building more times than I can count. This is not a project we are chasing for volume. It is one we are pursuing
                                     because it matters to me personally, and to our firm.


   ~ AeG1s
                                     We are teaming with Aegis Project Controls and their senior project director, Sean Flynn, PE, MBA, Sean brings 25 years of
                                     experience in historic stone restoration, preservation-sensitive cost analysis, and adaptive reuse - including work on the US
                                     Capitol, Arlington National Cemetery, the World War I Memorial, and the restoration of J.M. Pei's National Gallery of Art East
                                     Building. Together, our team covers every element of the City's stated scope: structural assessment, seismic evaluation, stabi lization
                                     concept development, and credible order-of-magnitude cost development grounded in real stone work.

                                     We are aware that prior engineering reports have recommended demolition, We respect that conclusion and look forward to
                                     forming our own independent view. Prior reports reflect the judgment of the engineers who wrote them, and the interests of those
                                     who commissioned them. Our job is to provide Cottonwood Heights with an honest, independent assessment - one that serves the
                                     City, not any particular outcome.

                                     Our commitment to this kind of work is not new, We found a path forward for the Macy's at the old Cottonwood Mall - a vacant
                                     anchor store just minutes from this site that most people had written off - converting it into a mixed-use development that serves
                                     the community today. We have stabilized historic mill structures when others had stopped trying. We have retrofitted unreinforced
                                     masonry churches from the early 1900s, preserved stone facades on civic landmarks, and given honest structural assessments to
                                     buildings on the Historic Buildings Registry that deserved more than a demolition bid. Old buildings are not a burden to us They
                                     are the projects we remember.

                                     We are preservation-optimists backed by structural rigor. We will bring creative thinking to this project, and honest cost context
                                     alongside it - because the goal is not to save this building at any price, The goal is to give it a fair chance. This community deserves
                                     to know what that chance actually looks like before anyone decides it is gone,

      WHY IT MATTERS.

      relevant to projects such as the Old Mill, where objective
      evaluation, realistic cost context, and sensitivity to
      preservation considerations are critical to supporting the
      City's decision-making process. Consistent with the firm's
      commitment to building things that matter, the goal is to
      provide clear, defensible recommendations that serve both
      the structure and the community it supports.

City's most prominent historic civic buildings.
Dunn Associates served as structural engineer
alongside HOK on a comprehensive seismic
renovation of this unreinforced masonry
structure. Our scope included seismic mass
analysis, URM out-of-plane wall anchorage
evaluation and detailing. foundation and column
attachment retrofit design, MEP seismic brilcing                                   ~

construction administration support The project
involved extensive review of existing drawings,

reflects our capability at the full spectrum of
historic masonry work - from initial assessment
through construction-phase support on a
landmark civic building.

           MORRISON & MERRILL


   This project is the closest scope analog to the
   Old Mill in our portfolio. The Morrison & Merrill
   Building is a three-story structure with partia I
   basement, constructed of wood framing with
   masonry exterior walls circa 1910. The owner
   engaged Dunn Associates to evaluate the
   building's anticipated seismic performance in
   connection with a potential sale. Our scope
   included on-site structural observations, review of
   existing drawings, development of seismic mass
   and loading conditions, and completion of a Tier
   1 checklist per ASC E 41-17. No destructive or
   non-destructive testing was included. Deliverables
   included a professional opinion summarizing
   findings and identifying structural deficiencies. This
   is precisely the type of assessment the Old Mill
   requires: observation-based, ASCE 41 framework,
   and honest findings,

                            HOLY CROSS (HAP,EL


        The Holy Cross Hospital Chapel is an
        approximately 4,000 SF two-story unreinforced
        masonry structure with wood-framed roof and
        floors, originally constructed in the early 1900s.
        Dunn Associates completed a Tier 1 seism ic
        evaluation under ASCE 41-17 in 2023, identifying
        overstressed masonry walls, insufficient diaphragm
        capacity, inadequate wall-to-diaphragm
        anchorage, and excessive wall slenderness ratios.
        We are now engaged for the full restoration scope
        - including settlement mitigation, brick and stone
        masonry repair, bel ltowe1 stabilization, diaphragm




                                                                                                                                      ..
        and wall anchorage upgrades, and selective w ~II
        reinforcement. The retrofit is voluntary, targeting
        Damage Control performance at the Design Basis

                             PICKLE & HIDE


    The Hide Building is an existing 15,000 SF
    two-story heavy timber and unreinforced
    masonry structure with a partial below-grade
    basement, located in Salt Lake City's Granary
    District. Dunn Associates is providing structural
    engineering for a voluntary full seismic retrofit of
    the existing building as part of a larger mixed-use
    development. The retrofit is being designed to full
    IBC forces per the International Existing Building
    Code, while preserving the building's historic
    character and keeping gravity modifications to a
    minimum. This project demonstrates our ability
    to work within the constraints of historic masonry
    while delivering a retrofit that meets current code
    performance levels.

                                                                    PROJECT TEAM
                                                                    Dunn Associates has assembled a focused team with the experience and
                                                                    expertise required to deliver a comprehensive structural assessment and
                                                                    preservation cost study for the Old Mill.

                                                                    Tait Ketcham, SE, as Principal in Charge, will provide technical oversight, lead
                                                                    the structural evaluation, and ensure quality and responsiveness t hroughout the
                                                                    project. Tyler Weinbrecht, PE, will seNe as Project Manager, coordinating
                                                                    day-to-day project execution, field investigation, and deliverable development.

                                                                    Dunn Associates is supported by Aegis Project Controls, with Sean Flynn, PE,
                                                                    MBA, serving as the historic stone and cost estimating specialist. Mr. Flynn brings
                                                                    extensive experience in preservation-sensitive cost analysis and historic stone
                                                                    construction, ensuring that stabilization and rehabilitation concepts are grounded
         Project Manager                                            in practical construction approaches and realistic cost expectations.

                                                                    Dunn Associates and Aegis Project Controls bring complementary experience
                                                                    in structural evaluation and preservation-sensitive cost analysis, allowing for
                                                                    coordinated, practical recommendations. Our team is experienced in field-based
                                                                    structural investigation of deteriorated and unsafe structures, including safe
                                                                    access planning and documentation.

                     OUR APPROACH



                         The Old Mil I is a multi-story stone masonry structure built between 1880 and 1883, constructed primarily of stacked
                         granite and native stone using mortar and gravity-based methods typical of industrial construction of that era. Following
                         a fire in 1893, only the exterior walls remained. The structure has experienced rehabilitat ion, extended vacancy, flood
                         events, and over a century of weathering. Prior engineering reports have recommended demolition.
                         Cottonwood Heights is not asking us to save the building. The City is asking for an honest, independent evaluation, one
                         that gives the building a fair hearing and equips decision-makers with the information they need to act responsibly.
                         That is exactly the kind of work we do, and it is the spirit in which we approach this engagement.




                       observe                                                  analyze                                                     recommend

   The Goldilocks Principle
                                                        Full preservation often costs more than stakeholders expect. Demolition often forecloses options that
   Find the middle ground.                              were more achievable than anyone real ized. The most useful thing an independent structural engineer
                                                        can do is balance preservation with real-world budget constra ints and give a beloved building the best
                                                        realistic chance at a meaningful future. That is what we are here to find.

DUNN ASSOCIATES, INC
Consu Iting Structural Engmeers



             May 29, 2026
             Cottonwood Heights City
             2277 Bengal Blvd
             Cottonwood Heights, UT 84121


             PROPOSAL FOR STRUCTURAL ENGINEERING SERVICES

             Project: 260084 - The Old Mill -     Preservation Feasibility Study
                        Cottonwood Heights, UT


             Dear Jared,


             Taking a fresh look at the Old Mill is exactly the kind of challenge we take on with real enthusiasm.
             This is the kind of project that matters, and we'd be honored to help Cottonwood Heights find a path
             forward.


             We have the team, availability, and focus to move quickly on this. Our structural engineers have direct
             experience evaluating historic masonry structures and will lead the site assessment and analysis
             personally.


             A few things that set our approach apart on a project like this:
                        •   Preservation-first thinking. Where others have seen a building past its prime, we've
                            learned to see possibility. We specialize in finding creative retrofit solutions that breathe
                            new life into existing structures -   including ones that have been written off.
                        •   Value-driven decision support. We present retrofit options with enough cost context that
                            stakeholders can weigh tradeoffs clearly and find the Goldilocks solution - one that
                            balances preservation ambition with real-world budget constraints.
                        •   Proven experience with historic structures. We've worked across a range of historic
                            building types and understand the structural, regulatory, and community dynamics that
                            come with this territory. We're happy to share examples of similar deliverables so you
                            know exactly what you're getting before we begin.
                        •   A personal stake in this project. Our CEO lives a couple of blocks from the Old Mill and is
                            personally invested in its preservation as a Cottonwood Heights resident. This isn't just a
                            project for us -   we'll approach it with the honesty and care it deserves, and we're
                            genuinely hopeful that this beautiful historic structure can find a new life.


              Project Overview: The Old Mill (Granite Paper Mill), built circa 1883, is a multi-story stone masonry
              structure located at the base of Big Cottonwood Canyon. The building has been vacant for an extended
              period, has sustained fire damage, and is in deteriorated condition. Prior engineering reports have
              recommended demolition; our work will take an independent, preservation-oriented approach -
              evaluating what it would take to stabilize, retrofit, and adaptively reuse the structure for public use and
              the costs to do so. Given limited existing documentation, our assessment will rely primarily on direct site
              observation and our team's struct•Jral expertise.




                                                                                                            Eno bling Grea1 Design'''
The scope of our services is as follows:


              1.   Condition Assessment - Site walk and documentation of existing conditions,
                   including structural deterioration, fire damage, and overall building integrity.
              2.   Tier 1 Structural Evaluation -   Mass and assess the existing stone masonry structure;
                   identify lateral deficiencies and key structural vulnerabilities beyond surface-level
                   conditions.
              3.   Concept Retrofit and Preservation Approaches -       Develop up to (3) three concept-
                   level solutions for stabilization and seismic retrofit, tailored toward adaptive reuse for
                   public use.
              4.   Cost and Development Recommendations -          For each conceptual preservation
                   approach, provide the following:
                        a.   Estimate Worksheet Summary
                             Preliminary cost worksheet summary based on current market pricing,
                             historical cost indexes, comparable project data, and appropriate
                             contingencies.
                        b.   Cost Evaluation and Recommendation
                             Estimated cost ranges for each structural item, along with a prioritized
                             recommendation based on urgency, structural risk, feasibility, and cost
                             impact.
                        c.   Development Opportunity Recommendation
                             A summary of potential value-add opportunities, including rehabilitation,
                             adaptive reuse, redevelopment potential, public-private partnership
                             opportunities, and impacts to property value or public benefit.
                        d.   Next Steps
                             Recommended actions for the City and Owner, such as contractor pricing,
                             additional engineering review, funding strategy, entitlement review, or
                           advancement into development planning.
              5.   Attendance and participation in Public Meetings - it is anticipated that we attend one
                   Planning Commission meeting (tentatively October 7) and one City Council meeting
                   (anticipated spring 2027) to present our findings, answer questions, etc.


                                                                      Fee: $18,500


If additional services are needed beyond the scope identified above - including additional attendance
at public meetings, coordination with a preservation group, or expanded structural analysis -         these can
be performed on an as-needed basis at an hourly rate. Such services must be requested in writing.


We thank you for the opportunity to provide you with this proposal. We have made our best attempt to
determine our scope for this project. However, if we have misinterpreted this scope in any way, please
let us know so adjustments can be made. Direction to proceed will constitute acceptance of this
proposal. A formal contract will follow as the structural fee is finalized. Thank you very much for your
consideration.


Respectfully yours,

                       Resolution 2026-41
       The city's leased fleet of police and public works vehicles
  is rotated every approximately two years. Because the vehicle
 lessor [here, Zions Bank] requires the city to purchase the fleet
 at the end of the lease term, the city requires the original seller
of the fleet [here, Garff Enterprises] to agree at the time of sale
    to repurchase the fleet at the end of the lease term, thereby
insulating the city from the repurchase risk. This resolution will
      approve the city's entry into that repurchase agreement.
                                   COTTONWOOD HEIGHTS

                                     RESOLUTION No. 2026-41


             A RESOLUTION APPROVING ENTRY TNTO A VEHICLE REPURCHASE
             OPTION AGREEMENT \VTTH GARFF ENTERPRISES, INC. FOR POLICE
               AND PUBLIC WORKS VEHICLES AND ASSOCIATED EQUIFMENT

        WHEREAS, the city of Cottonwood Heights ("City") formed City's police department and
its public works department to provide law enforcement and public works municipal services within
City, and desires to provide vehicles and associated equipment (collectively, the "Vehicles") for use
in performing those essential governmental functions; and

        WHEREAS, City desires to lease (rather than purchase) the Vehicles and have the option to
rotate the Vehicles about every two years to better assure dependability and reduced maintenance
costs; and

         WHEREAS, Zions Bancorporation, N.A. ("Zions") has proposed to lease the Vehicles to City
for a term of up to four years under that certain "Lease/Purchase Agreement" (the "Lease") between
Zions, as lessor, and City, as lessee, which will require City to purchase the Vehicles upon expiration
of the Lease, with City currently intending to effect such purchase between 1 July 2028 - 1 September
2028 (the "Repurchase Date"); and

       \VHEREAS, Garff Enterprises, Inc. ("Garff') owns one or more new motor vehicle
dealerships and desires to supply the Vehicles to Zions for City's ultimate use under the Lease; and

        WHEREAS, City is willing to enter into the Lease only if, inter alia, Zions irrevocably agrees
to purchase the Vehicles from Garff and Garff irrevocably agrees to repurchase from Zions or City,
as applicable, upon termination of the Lease, such of the Vehicles as City designates, as provided in
a ce1tain "Vehicle Repurchase Option Agreement" (the "Repurchase Agreement") contemplated
between City and Garff; and

       WHEREAS, Garff has expressed its willingness to enter into and perform under the
Repurchase Agreement to induce City to enter into the Lease with Zions and thereby to induce Zions
to purchase the Vehicles from Garff for lease to the City; and

        WHEREAS, City's municipal council (the "Council'') met in regular session on 16 June 2026
to consider, among other things, approving City's entry into the Repurchase Agreement; and

       WHEREAS, the Council has reviewed the form of the Repurchase Agreement, a photocopy
of which is annexed hereto; and

        WHEREAS, after careful consideration, the Council has determined that it is in the best
interests of City and its efficient administration, and the health, safety and welfare of City's residents,
to approve City's entry into the Repurchase Agreement as proposed;

       NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED by the city council of the city of Cottonwood
Heights as follows:
       Section 1.     Approval of Repurchase Agreement. The Repurchase Agreement is hereby
approved in substantially the form attached to this resolution, with such insertions, omissions and
changes as shall be approved by City's mayor in consultation with City's manager and attorney. The
execution of the Repurchase Agreement shall be conclusive evidence of such approval, and City's
mayor and recorder are hereby authorized and directed to execute and deliver the Repurchase
Agreement on City's behalf to the other parties thereto.

        Section 2.     Other Actions Authorized. City's officers and employees shall take all
actions necessary or reasonably required to carry out, give effect to and consummate the transactions
contemplated thereby, including, without limitation, the execution and delive1y of any and all other
documents required to be delivered in connection with the Repurchase Agreement.

       Section 3.     Appointment of Authorized Representatives. City' s manager and the
manager's designee in writing from time to time are hereby designated to act as authorized
representatives of City for purposes of the Repurchase Agreement until such time as the Council
designates any other or different authorized representative(s) for purposes of the Repurchase
Agreement.

        Section 4.     Severability. If any section, paragraph, clause or provision of this resolution
(this "Resoluhon") shall for any reason be held to be invalid or unenforceable, the invalidity or
unenforceability of such section, paragraph, or provision shall not affect any of the remaining
provisions of this Resolution.

        Section 5.     Repealer. All orders and resolutions or parts thereof, inconsistent herewith,
are hereby repealed to the extent only of such inconsistency. This repealer shall not be construed as
reviving any order, resolution or ordinance or part thereof.

       This Resolution, assigned no. 2026-41 , shall take effect immediately upon passage.

       PASSED AND APPROVED this 16 th day of June 2026.

                   Vehicle Repurchase Option Agreement
       THIS VEHICLE REPURCHASE OPTION AGREEMENT (this "Agreement") is
made effective 16 June 2026 between the city of COTTON\VOOD HEIGHTS, a Utah
municipality whose address is 2277 East Bengal Blvd., Cottonwood Heights, UT 8412 l ("City"),
and GARFF ENTERPRISES, INC., a Utah corporation whose address 111 East Broadway, Suite
900, Salt Lake City, UT 84111 ("Garff').



        A.      City is in the process of re-ontfitting its police department and its public works
depa11ment with approximately 51 motor vehicles for use by city employees in performing those
departments' governmental functions, which vehicles are particulaTly described on the attached
exhibit. The vehicles so described, together with any other mutually-agreed additions to such list,
are called the "Vehicles" in this Agreement.

       B.     City desires to lease (rather than purchase) the Vehicles and the option to rotate the
Vehicles every two years to better assure dependability and reduced maintenance costs.

        C.      Zions Bancorporation, N.A. or an affiliate ("J,essor") has proposed to lease the
Vehicles to City for a two-year tem1 under that certain "Lease/Purchase Agreement" (the "[,ease")
to be dated on or about 9 July 2026 between Lessor, as lessor, and City, as lessee. The Lease
effectively will result in the City's pmchase of the Vehicles at or before expiration of the Lease.
City cmrent!y anticipates that such repurchase likely will occur between l July 2028 - I September
2028 (the "Repurchase Date") even though the stated tem1 of the Lease extends to approximately
9 July 2029.

       D.     Garff owns one or more new motor vehicle dealerships and desires to supply the
Vehicles to Lessor for City's ultimate use.

        E.       City is willing to enter into the Lease only if, inter alia, (1) Lessor irrevocably
agrees to initially purchase the Vehicles from Garff, and (2) Garff inevocably agrees to repurchase
from Lessor (or from City, if required by the Lease) such of the Vehicles as City designates, at
City's option, for the Repurchase Price (defined below) on the Repurchase Date.

       F.      To induce City to enter into the Lease with Lessor, and to induce Lessor to
thereupon purchase the Vehicles from Garff, Garff desires to irrevocably agree to repurchase such
of the Vehicles as City designates, at City's option, for the Repurchase Price on the Repurchase
Date as specified in this Agreement.

        G.     The parties desire to set fm1h herein their entire agreement concerning the
repurchase of the Vehicles and all related dealings between City and Garff This Agreement shall
supersede all prior negotiations or agreements beti,veen the parties, oral and/or written, concerning
the subject matter of this Agreement.



       NOW, THEREFORE, in consideration of the premises, the mutual covenants and
undertakings of the parties hereto, and for other good and valuable consideration, the receipt and
sufficiency of which is hereby acknowledged, the parties hereto agree as follows:

        Section L        Vehicles. City hereby irrevocably agrees (a) to cause Lessor to purchase
from Garff or its affiliated dealership(s) the Vehicles specified on the attached exhibit (as the same
may be amended from time to time), and (b) to lease the Vehicles from Lessor as specified in the
Lease. Any Vehicles missing VINs on the attached exhibit will be identified upon their delivery
by Garff to City, and such exhibit shall be deemed amended accordingly without further action by
the parties.

       Section 2.     Dutv to Repurchase. To induce City to act as provided in section l above,
Garff hereby irrevocably agrees to repurchase from Lessor, City or any other seller under the Lease
such of the Vehicles as City designates, at City's option, on or after (as designated by City) the
Repurchase Date, subject to the following requirements:

               (a)     Vehicles Repurchased. The Vehicles to be repurchased to Garff hereunder
shall be those Vehicles designated by City, at City's option, from time to time, which may include
none, some or all of the Vehicles identified on the attached exhibit.

               (b)      Purchase Price. The Repurchase Price to be paid by Garff for all the
Vehicles totals $2,209,000.00. If City elects to require Garff to repurchase less than all the
Vehicles, then the total Repurchase Price to be paid by Garff shall be appropriately reduced by the
repurchase values shown on the attached exhibit in collllection with closing of Garff s repurchase
from City of the balance of the Vehicles.

               (c)      Mileage. Tfa Vehicle has over 33,000 odometer miles, the Repurchase Price
for that Vehicle shall be reduced by a charge of twenty cents ($.20) per excess mile.

               (d)    Condition. Each Vehicle shall be in condition to pass standard Utah State
motor vehicle safety and emissions inspections of the type annually required for each registered
motor vehicle. City may, at its option and cost, obtain and supply such current inspections to Garff
on the Repurchase Date. City shall be responsible for repairing, at City's cost, any defects caused
by City's use of a Vehicle which prevent the Vehicle from passing such inspections.

                (e)    Equipment. Each Vehicle is equipped with manufacturer-installed,
"factory" equipment ("Facto1y Equipment"); additional equipment installed by Vehicle Lighting
Solutions (such as police emergency lighting, sirens, prisoner cages, computer mounting
equipment, K-9 cages, weapons storage vaults) ("VJ,S Equipment"); and other equipment and
items, such as decals ("Other Items"). Each Vehicle shall be surrendered by City with all Factory
Equipment attached and in good working condition. All VLS Equipment and Other Items may be
removed from the Vehicles prior to their surrender at City's cost, for re-use on City's future
vehicles: radios and antennas.

                (t)   Bodv/Glass Damage. Body damage to a Vehicle (except for reasonable
wear), excessive holes not attributable to equipment installations previously agreed to by Garff,
and broken or chipped glass shall be repaired at City's cost.

               (g)    Closing. Closing of Garff s repurchase of Vehicles hereunder shall be at
such time, on such date, and at such place in Salt Lake County, Utah as City may specify upon at
                                                  2
least five business days' prior notice to Garff. Upon repurchase City shall provide Garff with
"clean titles" for each Vehicle repurchased; Vehicles with "salvage titles" or "branded titles" are
not eligible for repurchase.

        Section 3.     Possible Future Lease. Upon termination and satisfaction of the Lease,
Garff and City may elect to enter into a new !ease/re-purchase transaction with Lessor (or another
lessor) to provide a new pool of vehicles for City on such terms and conditions as may be mutually
agreed by such parties at that time, the parties acknowledging that pricing may be affected by
factors such as then-current interest rates and intervening variances in the cost of vehicles and
equipment.

       Section 4.     Condition Precedent. The performance of each party's obligations
hereunder is conditioned on full execution and delivery of the Lease by City and Lessor effectively
contemporaneously herewith.

                                   RESOLUTION No. 2026-42


   A RESOLUTION APPROVTNG ENTRY INTO A GOVERNMENTAL LEASE-PURCHASE
         AGREEMENT WITH ZIONS BANCORPORATION, N.A., FOR THE LEASE OF
         POLICE AND PUBLIC WORKS VEHICLES AND ASSOCIATED EQUIPMENT
         AND A UTHORIZTNG THE EXECUTION AND DELIVERY OF ALL RELATED
              DOCUMENTS AND THE TAKTNG Of ALL REQUIRED ACTIONS

       WHEREAS, the city of Cottonwood Heights (the "City") is a Utah municipality authorized
by Utah law to lease property for the benefit of the City and its residents and to enter into contracts
with respect thereto; and

       WHEREAS, the City formed its police department and its public works department to
provide police and public works municipal services within the City, and desires to purchase
vehicles and associated equipment (collectively, the "Vehicles") for those departments' use in
performing those essential governmental functions; and

       WHEREAS, in order to purchase the Vehicles, the City proposes to enter into a "Lease/
Purchase Agreement" (with all related documents and exhibits, the "Agreement") with Zions
Bancorporation, N.A. (the "Lessor"); and

       WHEREAS, the City's municipal council (the "Council") met in regular session on 16
June 2026 to consider, among other things, approving the City's entry into the Agreement; and

       \VHEREAS, the Council has Teviewed the form of the Agreement, a photocopy of which
is annexed hereto; and

        WHEREAS, after careful consideration, the Council has detem1ined that it is in the best
interests of the City and its efficient administration, and the health, safety and welfare of the City's
residents, to approve the City's entry into the Agreement as proposed;

       NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED by the city council of the city of Cottonwood
Heights as follows :

        Section l.     Approval of Agreement. The form, terms and provisions of the Agreement
are hereby approved in substantially the fom1 attached to this resolution (this "Resolutfon"), with
such insertions, omissions and changes as shall be approved by the City's mayor in consultation
with the City's manager, finance di.rector and attorney. The execution of the Agreement shall be
conclusive evidence of such approval, and the City's mayor and recorder are hereby authorized
and directed to execute and deliver the Agreement on the City's behalf to the other parties thereto.

        Section 2.      Other Actions Authorized. The City's officers and employees shall take
all actions necessary or reasonably required to carry out, give effect to and consummate the
transactions contemplated thereby, including, without limitation, the execution and delivery of
acceptance certificates, IRS forms and any tax certificate and agTeement and all other documents
as contemplated in the Agreement. The City's officers and employees are further authorized and
directed to take all actions necessary in conformity therewith, including, without limitation, the
